Water roads were neglected In fact, since the 1980s, this neglect has been increasing all the time. In the 1990s, as everyone knows, there were also neglected railway lines, and the waterways were forgotten. Indeed, there are three theoretical international waterways, water routes through Poland: the Vistula road, from east to west, from Rotterdam to Belarus, as well as the Kaliningrad road. Currently, the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Shipping, in cooperation with our Ministry - we have provided all the data - has prepared a programme for the development of waterways. This programme for the development of waterways will be presented to the Council of Ministers. It amounts to about 70 billion PLN, if I am not mistaken, I apologize if this information is inaccurate, inaccurate, because there were no details given when asking questions and I say it out of the question. It is planned there, among others. Odrzańska Waterway, is planned a water road on the Vistula with a channel connecting the Vistula to Bug, further east to Belarus and further east to Ukraine. The Danube - Odra, Odra - Elba and the Silesian Canal are also expected to connect the upper Vistula from Oświęcim to Gliwice Canal. As far as the route is concerned, this route has not yet been considered in detail. It was a phase of concept. The main problem with this channel will be the water supply of this channel. That's the main problem. The construction of such a channel can be compared to the construction of a highway. At the moment, the channels of the right width are being built, according to the class of the water road, i.e. about 60 meters long, sealed with asphaltetone, so that it looks very similar to the construction of the highway. I can still say that Poland is now joining the UN AGN Convention, the Convention on Waterways. Until now, Poland has not signed this convention. Therefore, it was not possible, among others. receive special resources for the development of waterways from the European Commission. These are special funds, but the condition for reaching for these funds is the inclusion of waterways in the AGN Convention, as well as in the TEN-T corridors. Nevertheless, I would like to say that at the moment there is about PLN 1 billion in the financial perspective for the development of waterways. These are among others. Renovation of Odrzańskie mucus, Oder regulations. This will, of course, continue and we will adapt... While the third class of the waterway was previously predicted, the fourth class of the waterway is still being referred to at the moment. The fourth class of the waterway is, you can say, the lowest class of the international waterway. They can be used for vessels with a capacity of about 1500.3000 t, i.e. about 30.60 containers. As far as transport-related waterways are concerned, only the fourth class of waterway is really the class that can meet transport conditions on a larger scale. As I said, a government document was created. It is currently being analysed and certainly the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Shipping will present this document. I would like to emphasise once again: we are working together in this area, because a large proportion of the investments in the development of waterways are linked equally to flood-related investments, among other things. anti-drying. That's where we'll be the lead side. On the other hand, when building channels, of course, the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Shipping will carry out these investments. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-19-sejm-19-2.u125	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! One of the first actions of the new management of the health department was leaning over the problems of Polish psychiatry. Pilotage will be conducted around 25 places in Poland. We've collected applications from centers that would like this piloting to take place on their territory. This has been analysed by the National Health Fund, because we want to know that these measures are able to cope with this task. We think that after the first half of the year we should already have some relevant data. I think we will be able to draw conclusions on how to implement this piloting for the whole country. So it seems that the project is very advanced. There will be no leaving environmental psychiatry in Poland. Thank you very much, Marshal.

We proceed with the examination of item 16 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law on anti-terrorist actions and amending certain other laws (Print No 516). We're waiting for the ministers. Please take the floor of the Secretary of State at the Chancellery of the Prime Minister Maciej Wąsik and present the reasons for the bill. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-19-sejm-19-2.u243	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Today I have the honour and duty to present to you on behalf of the Polish government a draft law on anti-terrorist activities. The main right of freedom is the right to security. If a man cannot feel safe, he cannot exercise his right to freedom. The Act on Anti-Terrorism is intended to ensure that Poland and Poles are more secure against the threat that has entered Europe strongly. Of course, we're talking about the plague of terrorism. Experiences from Madrid, London, and recently from Paris and Brussels are clear - at our borders there is a threat that is real and which is rapidly expanding. The Polish government cannot pretend not to see it. The government has prepared a law to give Polish services and police real tools to prevent and combat terrorist offences. What are the main principles of this law? This law is ordering the powers of the service. No more chaos. There will be a clear and clear division of roles between the head of the Home Office and the Home Minister. The Head of the ABW shall be the authority responsible for preventing terrorist incidents, the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration shall be the authority responsible for preparing, responding to and removing terrorist incidents. The ABW will coordinate analytical and information activities as well as operational and exploratory actions on terrorist threats. The Act introduces alarm degrees into the Polish legal order. These are degrees that correspond to NATO-WS terminology - Alpha, Bravo, Charlie, Delta, also in security and alarm issues in Polish cyberspace. The alarms will be introduced by the President of the Council of Ministers after consulting the Head of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs and the Head of the ABW. In cases of urgency, such powers will be exercised by the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration on his own. In the event of the introduction of any of the emergency steps, the Head of the ABW will be required to appoint a staff which, using the infrastructure that the ABW has, will direct all State action to combat crime and prosecute the perpetrators of terrorist offences. In the event of the introduction of a third or fourth level of alert, activation shall be carried out, preparation of the Armed Forces for action. When this third or fourth degree of alert occurs, it will be possible, with the permission of the Minister of National Defence, at the request of the Minister of Interior, to use the Armed Forces for police action. Use the Armed Forces not only when the police are insufficient, but when they may not be enough. Thanks to these records, the best Polish military special forces, the type of GROM, will be able to secure, for example, the NATO Summit or the World Youth Days. So far, they haven't practically had that possibility. In the event of the introduction of a third or fourth degree of alert, the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration will be able to prohibit public gatherings or mass events. I would like to stress that the decision of the competent authorities will still be subject to judicial review, so that it will be possible to appeal this decision to the court. Court control will be necessary here, because these are inalienable civil rights, such as the right to assembly. In the event of the introduction of at least the second stage of the alert, the Home Minister will be able to decide to limit or close traffic at border crossing points for a maximum period of 7 days. This will be a decision on immediate feasibility. It may now issue such a regulation, but it often takes several weeks to issue a regulation. The law will enhance the powers and powers of law enforcement services, but above all the prevention of terrorist crime. First of all, we need to be sure that the head of the ABW, as a key body for preventing terrorist crime, will have access to all the data that you collect in different bodies, in different registers, in databases. This access will be fast, on the way to teletransmission, but also fully accountable. It is an extremely strong strengthening of Polish services, it is an extremely strong strengthening, a large strengthening of the Anti-Terrorism Centre. One of the main powers that the Head of the ABW will be able to apply in order to prevent terrorist crime will be the possibility of using operational control ordered by the Head of the ABW, of which he will be required to inform the Attorney General and the Service Coordinator. This review by the Attorney General, when he thinks it's appropriate, will be able to stop at any time. This will be a review of only terrorist offences, only against non-Polish citizens and for a period of not more than 3 months. If there is a need to extend this check, the procedure shall be followed, which shall be laid down in the ABW Act, following the relevant consents of the Attorney General and the Court. The police and other services will also be given more authority to download fingerprint images and capture the facial image of a person who is not a citizen of the Republic of Poland - when there are doubts about the identity of that person when there is suspicion of illegal border crossing, when there will be doubts about the declared purpose of the stay, when there will be suspicion that the person has participated in terrorist training. The database in this area will be run by the chief police officer, as he conducts in this area, and will be made available for the purpose of prosecuting terrorist offences. The Minister of Home Affairs will also receive the powers of immediate expulsion from Poland of a foreigner who may conduct terrorist or spy activities. This will be a decision on immediate feasibility. Its appeal will, of course, be possible, while the appeal against this decision will already take place from outside the borders of Poland. We all remember a person who was expelled and walked the media for 3 months, slandering the Polish state. That's not gonna happen right now. Uniform services have long advocated the introduction of the possibility of special use of firearms, the so-called. Sniper shot. We introduce the possibility of using such a shot by the police, but also other services, in certain situations. In the context of terrorist incidents, a shot may be fired to save human life in order to disable a terrorist whose result may be death or serious health violation of that person. We introduce an appropriate procedure by law, removing responsibility from the sniper, transferring responsibility to his superiors. It is the right long-standing postulated by all Polish services, also known and introduced in the West of Europe. We also simplify the procedures using firearms in other situations than a sniper shot. In terrorist situations, there will be no need to use warnings that the police and other services must use before firearms are used at the moment. This is essential. I think it can be said in the category of oversight that the Internal Security Agency has not yet had any insight into the information covered by the banking secrecy. She only had insight into the investigation procedures, but we are now introducing the possibility of access to banking secrecy with the permission of the court. Often officers say that they know much better how money used to finance illegal enterprises circulates across Europe than what happens to them when they enter Poland. We also introduce the so-called institution at the operational level. A reverse spy and a reverse terrorist. The services will be able not to notify the prosecution in certain situations, subject to very strict conditions, if this is relevant in order to prevent a espionage or terrorist offence. We are also introducing regulations that will protect Polish cyberspace. We introduce the possibility for the Internal Security Agency to conduct tests of Polish critical infrastructure using methods used by criminals so that gaps and weaknesses can be found in ICT systems that support the most sensitive critical infrastructure. A register of incidents affecting ICT security will be kept at the Internal Security Agency. In special situations, the district court, at the written request of the Head of the ABW, submitted after obtaining the written consent of the Prosecutor General, will be able to order the blocking or requesting the administrator of the electronic system to block content on the Internet - content which is related to a terrorist offence. We are also introducing this law to amend the Code of Criminal Procedure. We introduce new institutions: an institution to search not a specific premises belonging to someone, but e.g. A certain area. This is what was introduced in France by means of an anti-terrorist law. We also introduce the possibility to carry out procedural activities between hours. 22 and 6 a.m., if this is a terrorist offence. We are introducing a new institution of a reduced standard of evidence in the event of an order to state allegations of a terrorist offence, if the good of conduct so requires. The statement of objections can, of course, be drawn up on the basis of information obtained as a result of operational and exploratory activities, e.g. in terrorist offences cases. information obtained by interview or by partner services. In this situation, the court, I stress, at the request of the prosecutor against the person charged, will be able to apply temporary detention for a period not exceeding 14 days. But, I stress, it will be a court decision. The law also amends the provisions of the Penal Code, thus fulfilling our international obligations, such as those arising from the Russian Convention. It supports the gradual preparations for terrorist offences - not only attempted and carried out, but also the preparation will already be criminal. It also introduces into the Polish Penal Code new crimes, which the lynx convention is ordering us to introduce, i.e., a crime of participation in training that may enable a terrorist offence to be committed, as well as the crossing of the state border in order to commit a terrorist offence. The Code also tightens sanctions in case of conviction for the crime of false alarm, which is a plague in Poland. A number of false alarms are recorded every day in Warsaw. Now the court will have to rule on a settlement and a cash benefit of no less than 20,000 at the time of the conviction of the person who is guilty of the offence. zł. We are also introducing a coroner's witness institution in relation to a terrorist who voluntarily withdrew from a terrorist offence and revealed to the law enforcement authority the relevant circumstances of the act or prevented it from being committed. This person, at the request of the prosecutor, will not be subject to penalty by a court decision. This is a very important right, a very important rule breaking down the solidarity of criminal groups. Finally, the bill faces new technologies that appear, which were already a threat to Polish air traffic - it is about drones. It gives the services, in certain situations, the possibility of taking control, immobilizing or shooting down the drone - of course in certain situations, strictly described in this law. The Act also amends the telecoms law. We follow the West, eliminate the anonymous prepaid service - no more anonymity for those who try to commit crimes. I would like to say that, in principle, the whole West, the whole European Union, is leaving this type of service. In principle, the main EU countries have long given up the possibility of maintaining an anonymous prepaid service on the telecommunications market. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! All these powers I have mentioned here are intended to ensure that the Internal Security Agency, the police and other services effectively protect Poland from terrorist threats. We want Poland and Polish services to be a strong element in the European system for preventing and combating terrorism. We also want to ask a lie to the proverb: a wise Pole after harm. We learn from those who have paid an extremely high price for their lack of imagination, for their disregard for certain dangers. This law is so that we never have to experience what has led European countries, Western European countries, to adopt similar rules at home. We believe as a government that there is no other way. Therefore, on behalf of the Polish government, I have the honour to stand before you here today and submit for adoption a bill on anti-terrorist actions. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-19-sejm-19-2.u363	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a government bill amending the Customs Law Act and some other laws contained in print no. 513. The proposed law amending the Customs Law and certain other laws serves to apply the Union Customs Code established by Regulation No 952 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 9 October 2013 and the provisions implementing its provisions. The new Union customs rules shall apply from 1 May 2016. However, they require the adaptation of national legislation, which was only possible after the publication by the European Union of parts of the rules implementing the EU Customs Code in March this year. The draft amendment to the Customs Law presented to the High House is an important element of this adjustment. High Izbo! The EU Customs Code has replaced the Community Customs Code, which has been in force for more than 20 years, based on the integration of customs procedures applied separately by individual Member States in the 1980s. Since the entry into force of the Community Customs Code has been amended several times and significantly to address certain problems, including: protecting good faith or taking into account security requirements and following major changes in both EU and international legislation. In 2011, it was decided to transform the modernised customs code into the Union Customs Code. The main objective of the Union Customs Code being implemented is to improve the application of customs rules in the European Union and to computerise customs operations. The achievement of this basic objective should ensure, first, that legal trade and combating fraud are facilitated by the definition of simple and economically justified customs procedures and, second, that a balance is struck between the duty of customs authorities to ensure the correct application of customs rules and the right of traders to fair treatment, thirdly, harmonised and harmonised application of customs controls by the Member States of the Union in order to ensure an equivalent level of controls throughout the Union, so as to prevent conduct that distorts competition, fourthly, the same facilitation for traders in all Member States mainly by making the information and communication systems used for handling customs operations available, fifthly giving customs authorities a leading role in the supply chain due to their participation in monitoring and management of international trade. High Izbo! In accordance with the standard set out in Article. 3 (1) (a) 1 (a) (i) (ii) (ii) (iii) (ii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) and the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union has the so-called "Union'. exclusive competence in the area of customs union, which means that only the European Union can constitute law and adopt legally binding acts in this area. Member States may do so only under the authority of the Union or for the purpose of implementing Union acts. This is what the Republic of Poland does in the Customs Law Act. The provisions of this law are complementary to EU customs legislation and therefore the substance of the draft amendment to customs legislation submitted to the High House is to ensure the application of the EU Customs Code and its implementing rules. Customs law in force is amended by adapting the names of the customs authorities to the Union Customs Code, replacing the provisions of the Community Customs Code established by the relevant provisions of the Union Customs Code, repealing national provisions incompatible with the new arrangements contained in the Union Customs Code and introducing new arrangements for the implementation of Union Customs Regulations. The substantive amendments to the Customs Law introduced by this Act also include provisions concerning customs procedures. Until now, Section IV of the Tax Order applies mutatis mutandis to customs proceedings, taking into account the changes resulting from customs legislation. Proposed in the new wording of the Article. 73. 1 Customs Law The concept is to significantly restrict the application of the Tax Order to customs proceedings. The need to limit the application to customs of the provisions of the Act is due both to the establishment at Union level of detailed procedural rules and to the views of the Court of Justice of the European Union, which is the position of uniform application of customs law in the customs territory of the European Union. The powers of legal entities directly conferred by Union law in individual Member States would be different if the concepts and deadlines of Union law were defined in national legal orders. It should be added that changes in customs procedures are also expected by traders who thus receive a much simpler, tailored to the specifics of trade in goods and to a large extent harmonised set of rules for handling customs before customs authorities throughout the Union's customs territory. Further important changes requiring attention are the introduction of a statutory provision giving rise to the authorisation of use of the TIR procedure and the authorisation to simplify the acceptance of goods under that procedure (Article). 17 (a) and the introduction of a simplified way of regulating the situation of goods in the case of minor smuggling offences. According to the proposed solution, the perpetrator of the offence after the adoption of the criminal mandate will be informed by the customs authority of the possibility of renunciation of the illegally introduced goods to the Treasury. Where tobacco products and medicinal products are approved in this way, and under certain conditions alcoholic beverages and cosmetics, in accordance with Article. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4 and 5 Customs laws will be destroyed without the need for a long-term procedure for the court to rule on the forfeiture of goods in favour of the Treasury. The draft law on the amendment of the Customs Law and certain other laws amends, in addition to the Customs Law, 21 other laws, including the Goods and Services Tax Act, the Excise Tax Act or the Customs Service Act. Amendments to these laws are, in principle, adapted to customs legislation. In view of the interest of entrepreneurs, I would like to draw attention to the change in the Trade Tax Act introducing joint and several liability for direct representatives within the meaning of customs rules, i.e. those acting on behalf of and for the benefit of importers. In principle, when imported goods are covered by certain simplified procedures, importers have the right to settle VAT directly in a tax return without having to pay it to the customs office, which means settlement without financial involvement. Such a privilege should be enjoyed by taxable persons who are authorised to use simplified procedures, as this is in principle a privilege addressed to reliable importers verified on the basis of objective criteria. In cases where imports are made using the direct representative's licence, most often the customs agency, the represented entities are not subject to verification, inter alia. in terms of solvency, as is the case with the holder of the said licence. The statistics show that more than twice as many customs declarations in simplified procedures are made to importers benefiting from VAT clearance in declarations by direct representatives - in relation to those made by importers themselves. This means that the condition for the exercise of this right by the entity verified as reliable is not fulfilled in respect of most operations using the possibility of clearing VAT in the declaration. The introduction of joint and several liability of direct representatives, where the taxable importer does not apply for such authorisation, in fact leads to a levelling of the situation in which the indirect agent currently has co-accounting for VAT, and provides mechanisms to safeguard the interests of the State budget, without causing undue burden on economic operators. I would like to stress that the solution put in place will in no way change the approach to clearing by importers holding simplified procedures, even if they benefit from the institutions of direct representation. However, this change will contribute to a clear definition of the rules in which cases joint and several liability for unpaid tax is to be borne. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! When passing the bill to the High House for consideration, I pay particular attention to the date of its entry into force. The Act should enter into force as soon as possible, as from 1 May this year, i.e. from the date of application of the Union Customs Code, there is no consistency between Union customs law and national customs law. As I mentioned at the outset, the government received published legislation from the European Union implementing the EU Customs Code in March this year, which decided on the way in which the national rules should be adapted. I therefore ask the High House to consider the bill at the optimal time and assure the government that we are ready to participate in the parliamentary work with full commitment. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-19-sejm-19-2.u365	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The Community Customs Code was established in 2008 as part of the customs reform, but outside its Article. 30 - it was not used due to the lack of implementing rules. In 2011, it was decided to transform the modernised customs code into the EU Customs Code due to the need to implement the changes resulting from the Lisbon Treaty. In this way, the Union Customs Code was established in 2013. The ratio legis of this revision of customs legislation indicates the simplification and acceleration of customs operations as well as the sealing of the entire customs system of the European Union. This is to be achieved through the computerisation of customs turnover in each Member State of the European Union, with the aim of the European Union to move completely away from paper documents for electronic infrastructure based on specialised IT systems. I do not go into these changes, because the general changes and provisions of the EU Customs Code mentioned in the preamble have been discussed by the Minister. There are no very detailed descriptions at this point in time, but I would like and allow myself to draw attention to the benefits resulting from this, also for entrepreneurs, because today the regulations introduced by the EU Customs Code authorise entrepreneurs with status. Additional conditions on which the granting of this status will depend, but also on customs debt and security, are indicated. It is about the possibility of using a reduced security and increasing the expiry of a customs debt. In the case of customs proceedings, there will be harmonisation of the procedures of the customs authorities of the Union in relation to the issuing of decisions. Finally, in the case of simplified procedures, the amendments concern the simplified customs declaration institution and the entry in the register of the declarant, as well as new facilitations such as customs self-service and the fact that the customs authority, at the request of an authorised economic operator of status, may allow certain customs formalities to be carried out by the trader. If we can talk about any inconvenience, it will probably result from complicated procedures related to the legal regime of the transitional period during which Member States will be obliged to adapt their existing customs infrastructure to the requirements of the EU Code. However, the implementation of all the information systems envisaged should allow for the smooth operation of individual institutions to function together as a modern, efficient EU customs system. Probably one of the most important - if not the most important - effects of amending the laws will be simplification of customs procedures. The Act includes the authorisation to use simplified procedures for covering goods under the TIR procedure, which concerns the international carriage of goods. In practice, transit formalities will be reduced to a minimum for reliable entrepreneurs. Therefore, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice fully supports the draft law submitted to the government. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-19-sejm-19-2.u371	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I would like to present on behalf of the club Moderna a position concerning the draft law from print no. 513 on the amendment of the Customs Law Act and some other laws. Polish customs law changed very significantly at the time of Poland's accession to the European Union on 1 May 2004, which is linked to Poland's accession to the customs union created by the Member States of the Union. The Customs Code so far in force and all implementing regulations issued pursuant to it have ceased to apply. The Customs Union is the basis of the European Union. In order to ensure the free movement of goods at the initiative of the European Parliament, the Council of Europe and the European Commission, a law is being established laying down the rules for the functioning of the customs union. Such a document is the EU Customs Code. With its establishment, the earlier provisions of the European Community, such as the Community Customs Code and its complementary Regulations, have ceased to apply. The main political objective of the Union Customs Code is to improve the functioning of the customs union as a whole through uniform application of customs rules throughout the customs territory of the European Union and by the national customs services of the Member States. The EU Customs Code provides for a number of changes in Polish legislation which should be adapted to the law in force within the European Union, for example in matters of customs debt and security, the status of authorised economic operator or the storage of goods. Furthermore, any simplification of the customs process provided for in the Code, including centralised clearance and self-service, is extremely important. The purpose of the amendments to Customs Law and other laws notified in print No 513 is to align the provisions in force in our country with those in force in the European Union. Therefore, part of the provisions introduces cosmetic changes, replacing the word. The word "Union" and some of it goes further in adapting Polish law to the EU Customs Code. The entry into force of the draft law amending the Customs Law and certain other laws will not result in a reduction in revenue and will not result in an increase in expenditure in the public finance sector, including in the budget of the state and local government. National regulation involves reducing excessive administrative tasks in certain areas and shifting forces and measures to improve the efficiency of audit powers for authorised entrepreneurs or introducing new solutions, in particular for centralised clearance. The efficiency and cost of activities of tax administrations will not change. Furthermore, it is worth mentioning, because this is a very rare case, it is very rare for the current government that the bill has been consulted with stakeholders who have raised a number of concerns about it. The parties replied to the doubts. Ladies and gentlemen, if each law were so widely consulted with stakeholders, perhaps the European Commission would not have implemented the rule of law procedure for our country. Therefore, on behalf of the Modern Club at first reading, I propose that the project be directed to committee work. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-19-sejm-19-2.u373	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club, I will present my position on the draft Customs Law Act and some other laws, print no. 513. The draft law lays down the rules for the implementation and application of the Union Customs Code established by the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council. The Code entered into force on 30 October 2013 and is applicable from 1 May 2013. The main objective of the Union Customs Code is to improve the functioning of the customs union as a whole. It is the uniform application of customs rules throughout the Union by the national customs services of the Member States. The Union Customs Code focuses on the designation of strategic directions and objectives in customs law. Code regulations will be applied in Polish almost directly. The Code also applies the Commission Delegated Regulations and Implementing Regulations which together form a package of the Union Customs Code. One of the most important regulations introduced by the EU Customs Code is the exchange of information in the general application of IT techniques. The exchange of information and its storage and data exchange will take place through electronic data processing techniques to be implemented by the end of 2020. In the end, all customs and commercial transactions will be carried out electronically. This will be done through a special platform of electronic customs services, will of course be with an honored electronic signature, the qualified whether on a trusted ePUAP profile, but also with an electronic signature verified by the Customs Service itself certificate. The draft law introducing the EU Customs Code amends our Customs Law, as well as 20 other laws. On the basis of the Treaty of Lisbon, the Code sets out names and definitions by harmonising their content in all legal provisions. The Code introduces numerous simplifications in customs procedures and generally limits their number. One of the most important effects of the amended legislation will be the simplification of customs procedures. The law includes the authorisation to use simplified procedures for covering goods under the TIR procedure. In the case of reliable entrepreneurs, all formalities will be significantly reduced. With regard to authorised economic operators, the Code indicates additional conditions on which the granting of the status of authorised economic operator will depend and, what is important, extends the catalogue of benefits available only to holders of the status of authorised economic operator. An authorised entrepreneur will be able to do this, among others. the provision of services of a customs representative in a Member State other than that in which he is established shall be entitled to a reduced comprehensive security, shall be granted a centralised clearance and a so-called clearance. customs self-service. In addition, the Customs Code amends the application of simplifications to customs declarations, e.g. the customs declaration may also be lodged in the form of an entry in the register kept by the declarant. A new simplification under EU law is also the already mentioned centralised clearance and customs self-service. The nature of the centralised check-in shall consist of the possibility of customs declaration of goods at the customs office competent for the place of establishment of the declarant, which shall be presented at another customs office. With regard to customs self-service, it is simply possible for certain activities to be carried out by the trader himself in order to determine the amount and control under customs supervision. Today's draft law introduces detailed provisions of the EU Customs Code. The material is complex and extensive. Introducing it should have a positive impact on the competitiveness of businesses that operate in the common market under conditions [[♪ The bell ♪]] simplification of electronic customs procedures and unification of law in all Member States. The Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party is in favour of transferring the project to the committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-19-sejm-19-2.u387	Mrs. Marshal! Dear Minister, High Izbo! As a representative of the group of Members of the applicants, I am pleased to present to you a draft law amending the Act on Renewable Energy Sources, Parliamentary Printing No 476. The draft Act amending the Act on Renewable Energy Sources and some other laws removes first of all interpretation, legal and editorial doubts regarding the provisions contained in the Act of 20 February 2015 on Renewable Energy Sources, introduces tools for controlling the energy mix, separates the role of prosumer from the energy producer and extends the definition of prosumer. I would like to present to you the fundamental changes in the RES system. This is the introduction of transparent rules for the generation and use of electricity from RES for own use in micro-installations, provided that such actions are not directed towards achieving profits, but only directly to meeting their own energy needs. We also propose reducing information obligations on the part of energy producers in micro-installations. Another thing that will change is the abolition of manufacturers who failed to fulfil their information obligations before the Act came into force. We would also like to guarantee consumer rights to prosumers. We envisage changing the definition of RES installation and introducing a new definition of hybrid RES installation. Changes in the auction support system are also part of the change in the RES installation in the allocation of auctions into technological baskets and the promotion of technologies that generate energy in a stable and predictable manner. We want to introduce opportunities for more efficient use of locally available biomass resources. We want to eliminate the unnecessary elements contained in the statements made by energy producers in RES installations using biomass, biogas, agricultural biogas or bioliquids and eliminate the risk of taking all the support in the situation of generation in the above-mentioned installations of parts of energy based on other fuels. We would like to introduce the possibility of building modern dedicated multi-fuel combustion plants. The amendment will also include details of the issues related to the permissible value of public aid. Instead of the name of the Renewable Energy Settlement Operator, we would like to propose the name of the Settlement Manager SA. We also propose the introduction of the so-called test procedure. the incentive effect for agricultural biogas producers. When it comes to changing the names... We propose, as regards prosumer, that the draft regulation should form the basis for a competitive, safe and sustainable renewable energy market and that the existing competitive rules and the high level of protection of the addressees of the regulation, including consumers, should therefore be strengthened. We propose to waive the support mechanism for prosumers based on the distribution of public aid. Prosumers are end-users who are simultaneously micro-producers producing electricity for their own use. We would like to extend the definition of prosumer, including local self-government units, organisations, religious unions, other end-users, producing energy for their own needs. As regards, I ask you, a substantial matter, the withdrawal of the prosumer support mechanism - it is based on the distribution of public aid - it is precisely that, as this mechanism is based on the distribution of public aid, we propose a completely different settlement and withdrawal from the existing mechanism relating to prosumer settlements. Prosumers are end-users who are simultaneously micro-producers producing electricity for their own use. Such a simple definition of prosumers will lead to the development of this segment of the RES market without having to bear the burden from other end-users. It should be noted that the construction, which was to enter into force, contained in Chapter 4 of the RES Act, consisting of a fixed guaranteed tariff, the value of which amounted to PLN 0.75 per kWh, would entail a disproportionate financial burden, in particular by other end-users. The price of 1 MWh produced in this installation of electricity would amount to PLN 750 at the market price oscillating around PLN 170. At the stage of work on this law, on the RES Act, and the implementing acts for this regulation, we cooperated with the Ministry of Economy and just the Ministry of Economy estimated the annual cost of supporting micro-installations using a fixed guaranteed price of PLN 94 million in the first year for 25% of the volume of 800 MW admitted to this system. This means that the possible level of support for the fixed price guaranteed by regulation would be around PLN 400 million. Hence the change of approach to prosumer support. It is envisaged that the difference between the amount of electricity put into the grid and the amount of electricity collected from that network should be settled in the following way: from 1 to 0,7 in the case of electricity produced in micro-installations with a total installed power of not more than 7 kW, from 1 to 0,5 in the case of electricity produced in micro-installations with a total installed power capacity of more than 7 kW and - the third option - from 1 to 0,35 in the case of electricity produced in micro-installations for which the prosumer used public support for the purchase or installation of micro-installations or parts thereof. As far as this mechanism is concerned, it assumes the possibility for Prosumer to settle the amount of electricity put into the grid and the amount of electricity collected from that network over 365 days. This is the settlement in the form of a discount. At the same time, it is laid down in the legislation that the energy clearance by Prosumer does not constitute an economic activity and is not a service provision or sale within the meaning of the Goods and Services Tax Act. In our novel there is also a definition of micro- and macro-classes of energy. The concept of micro- and macro-class energy is another element of the task of ensuring energy security both nationally and locally. Micro- and macro-classes as civil-law stakeholder agreements consisting of natural persons, legal persons, entities without legal personality, scientific units, R &amp; D units or local self-government units are intended primarily to integrate local environments and resources to build fuel and energy generation facilities or to make more efficient use of locally available energy infrastructure. It is about creating self-sufficient energy areas. In accordance with the added editorial unit in Article. 38a in the draft law, economic activity within a micro- or macro-class of energy may be carried out on the basis of a concession for an entity which coordinates that part of the Joint Undertaking on the basis of a contract with other entities. The appropriate interaction of the electricity distribution network operator with which the micro- or macro-classer intends to cooperate shall be guaranteed by its obligation to conclude a distribution service contract with the applicant for connection to that network on an equal basis where there are technical and economic conditions for connection to the grid and distribution of energy and the applicant for connection to the network meets the conditions for connection to the network. Even if the above conditions are not met, the distribution network operator may refuse to conclude the contract, but the refusal shall be made in writing together with the reasons for it. Such refusal shall be sent to the applicant for the conclusion of a distribution contract and the President of the Energy Regulatory Office shall be informed of the refusal. If there is a dispute in this situation, the President of the URE shall decide on the refusal of the agreement at the request of the party. The President of the URE shall be provided with an additional legal instrument as he may, at the request of one of the parties, issue a provision setting out the conditions for the distribution of energy until the dispute is finally resolved. The proposed revision of the definition of RES installation aims to eliminate the problems raised by investors under the existing definition of one connection point and the generation of electricity or heat from one type of renewable energy source. This definition of RES installation will contribute to the elimination of RES development barriers and will reduce investment costs, which would in some cases be higher if the investment were implemented, taking into account the previous wording of the above definition. In addition, a supplement to this article is proposed. 2 of the RES Act, a dictionary with a definition of a hybrid RES installation, which is understood as a team of at least two RES installations, which differ in the characteristics of the energy supply and which form, as a result of the connection, a consistent electrical and area-wide set providing the customer with permanent access to electricity according to the quality requirements laid down in the provisions of the Energy Law or in a comprehensive agreement concluded with the recipient. Such an installation unit can also be supported by an energy storage facility and then the electricity supplied from it is treated as energy from a renewable energy source. This proposal, combined with further proposals for changes in the auction support system, aims to promote installations which, through the integration of different RES sources, will have greater stability in power generation. As far as the auction system is concerned, it is proposed to introduce technological baskets/groups in order to make the mix of RES more efficient, taking into account current demand and energy security issues. It is about the need to generate energy in a stable and predictable way. In view of the above, the project envisages introducing new rules for determining the maximum amount and value of electricity from renewable energy sources, which can be sold through auctions in the following calendar year by taking into account specific technological mixes. According to the auction project, electricity produced in RES installations is sold separately. First of all, the extent to which the installed electrical capacity is used, regardless of source of origin, greater than 3504 MWh/MW/year. Point 2: Using biodegradable part of industrial and municipal waste, of plant or animal origin, including waste from waste treatment facilities and waste from water treatment and waste water treatment, in particular sewage sludge, in accordance with the waste legislation on the qualification of the part of energy recovered from thermal treatment of waste. Here, too, there will be a situation where CO2 emissions are not greater than 100 kg/MWh, with a capacity utilisation rate greater than 3504 MWh/MW/year. It should also be pointed out here that the issue concerns the members of the energy macroclass, point 4, as well as the members of the microclass. And there are other cases than those mentioned in these points, but I do not go deeper into the detail of this topic. Changes in the auction support scheme also provide for the possibility to participate in the hybrid RES auction. The aim of this action is to make full use of synergies between RES installations using different types of RES for electricity generation, so that energy generation can be stable and predictable over a longer period of time. The objective of the definition of local biomass is to ensure the efficient use of locally available biomass resources. The analysis of current trends in energy use of biomass shows that the existing provisions introduced for the use of locally available resources, in particular residues or waste from agricultural production and the agri-food sector, do not fully contribute to the objectives pursued. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 119a of the draft law indicates that the Minister responsible for Energy may define by regulation a radius of the area from which local biomass may be obtained, no more than 300 km from the manufacturing unit in which it will be used, taking into account the need for efficient use of locally available biomass resources. Until the publication of the abovementioned document. the regulation, the radius of the area from which the local biomass may be obtained, shall not exceed 300 km from the manufacturing unit in which it is used. In other cases, please, it should also be said that simplification also applies to statements made by energy producers in RES installations using biomass, biogas, agricultural biogas or bioliquids. The project provides for the elimination of the need to indicate that biomass used does not contain substances that do not naturally occur in a particular type of biomass, due to the problematic nature of the regulation and the impossibility of avoiding minor impurities. In addition, the changes made eliminate the risk of taking all support from the situation of production in the above mentioned. the installation of energy components based on other fuels by indicating, inter alia, that the certificate of origin does not qualify for that part of the electricity used, etc., and, which should be stressed that, according to the wording of the statements in question so far, any slightest failure in terms of the fuels used has led to the fact that all energy could not be classified as renewable energy. By introducing the proposed solutions it will be possible to exclude only the volume of energy that has been produced on the basis of other fuels. The draft amendment also includes provisions that will enable the creation of new dedicated multi-fuel combustion plants and their participation in the auction system to promote the creation of electricity generation facilities in a stable manner, as well as the creation of an increased demand for locally available biomass resources. In view of the above, a number of changes have been made to allow participation in the auction system of the entities concerned. These changes include, in particular, the revision of the definition of a dedicated multi-fuel combustion plant, the introduction of the need to set the reference price for these installations, and the exclusion of such installations from the restrictions which so far have been contained in Article. Article 44 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 8 RES Act. The project also proposes to entrust tasks resulting from the RES Act to a stable entity with the necessary experience in the performance of similar tasks in the electricity sector - it is about the Management Board of Resolutions SA - and the liquidation of the company located only at the initial stage of the organization - it is about the Operator of Resolutions of Renewable Energy SA. By exploiting synergies, this will reduce the operating costs of the system to a minimum and improve financial management. As far as matters related to the Settlement Manager are concerned, I would like to add that the Settlement Manager has successfully since 2008 been engaged in obtaining financing and clearing of public aid in the context of the process of granting stranded costs and gas costs from the appropriations resulting from the transitional levy under the Act of 29 June 2007 on the rules for covering costs incurred by manufacturers in connection with the early termination of long-term contracts for the sale of power and electricity. The obligation to provide support under the RES Act will also ensure that this system does not generate additional organisational and operational costs, and the paying agent will perform these tasks efficiently due to the experience gained so far. This will be due to the fact that the entity responsible for the collection of the RES charge will be the TSO providing the provision service of the national electricity system - KSE, and the payer of the RES fee will be the RES using this service. As part of the process, technical and organisational solutions will be used - here RES payers are, at the same time, payers of the transitional fee - already used for the purpose of collecting the transitional fee under the PPAs Act, thus avoiding additional costs which would entail the independent conduct of this process by the Renewable Energy Settlement Operator SA, requiring the construction of communication and financial channels from scratch with each RES payer individually. Synergies of financial management, organisational capacity and well-established position will be exploited. The risk to the smooth start of the new support scheme, linked to the creation of a new entity from scratch and the need for organisational efficiency, shall be avoided. Here it may be stressed that entrusting the function of the renewable energy billing operator of the Disclaimer SA will allow, following the introduction of appropriate amendments to the Act on PPAs, also for the RES support system tested under the Act on PPAs to obtain external financing in the event of a temporary shortage of funds in the RES support system. The effective use of this mechanism is only possible in the case of an entity with a sufficiently long credit history and acquired credibility. This is the case in the case of the Account Holder SA. In addition, entrusting the implementation of two support schemes of the Settlement Manager SA will, subject to certain conditions, avoid additional financial costs where a deficit of funds is incurred in the account of one fee and their surplus will occur in the account of the second fee. The PPAs Act introduces a number of ownership restrictions, ensuring the full transparency of the management and control of the fund. According to Art. Article 49 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 of the Act on PPAs profit The Management Board of Settlements SA can only be used to finance the activities specified in this Act. The principles of supervision exercised by the Minister responsible for Energy over the company of the Management Board of Settlements SA are laid down by law. The management of the Settlements SA has been implementing a public aid scheme under the PPAs Act since 2008, and the tasks of the company are to collect, manage and pay the funds to the generators covered by the public aid scheme under the rules set out in the PPAs Act, i.e. activities similar to those foreseen for OREO SA. The company's activities are classified as other financial services activities, not elsewhere classified, excluding insurance and pension funds. To sum up the above, the cheapest and most effective way for the SR SA to enter into the rights and obligations of OREO SA with due regard to the interest of the State Treasury, employees and consequences of liabilities vis-à-vis OREO SA's counterparties was chosen. In addition, in the article. 47a an obligation to confirm the so-called the incentive effect for agricultural biogas producers, according to which the manufacturer, who intends to produce agricultural biogas in RES installations and wants to obtain a certificate of origin for agricultural biogas, is required to submit an application to the President of the URE for official confirmation of the incentive effect. The aim of this provision is to fill the legal gap in the scope of the so-called law. the incentive effect for those manufacturers which are not subject to the concession obligation. This provision aims to ensure consistency of regulation with EU state aid rules. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-19-sejm-19-2.u403	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I am honored for the first time to present my position on the parliamentary draft law amending the Act on Renewable Energy Sources and some other laws, print No 476. Poland has exceeded halfway through its obligations under EU Directive 2009/28/EC on the promotion of the production of energy from renewable sources. By the end of 2014, we will reach 11.45% share of renewable energy in a perspective of 15,85% by 2020 in accordance with the national action plan. However, the achievement of the minimum objective is not so sure. Already, the energy sector is asking questions about the possibility of developing RES in the three sectors that the European Union accounts for, i.e. heating and cooling, electricity and transport. Further disregarding the real problem is increasingly bringing Poland closer to failing to fulfil its commitments by 2020. It is estimated that about 3% of the share of energy from RES in total gross energy consumption, i.e. 30% below the target. Such a green energy deficit can have two consequences. The first is the need to carry out a statistical transfer of energy from other countries, the cost of which may be about PLN 8 billion. In the second case, infringement of EU commitments will be brought against Poland, which may result in financial penalties imposed on Poland. It is therefore extremely urgent to submit this bill to the High House, to take up legislative work and to adopt a law that will trigger the long-awaited possibility for citizens to invest in renewable energy sources. In the draft law, the power-generating entity was called prosumer, which also means the energy producer and consumer. The prosumer will be e.g. natural person, school, clinic, farmer, housing community, company and many other entities that will generate energy in installations up to 40 kW for their own needs, but not for sale. The surplus of energy will be released to the network and then collected in the annual settlement, so-called. net metering, in certain quantities, depending on the size of the installation or the public aid received. Thus, the energy grid will be an energy warehouse for prosumers. After storing the energy, the prosumer will receive the relative amount specified in the Act. Since there will be no sale of energy, there will be no tax. Hybrid installations may be used, e.g. windmill plus photovoltaic panels. Prosumer will not have to own the installation. It is enough that he will be a user, which will open up wide funding opportunities. In principle, any counter will be able to form the basis for the operation of prosumer energy, there will be no limit on the number of installations or the time limit for entering the new system, there will be no limit on the total capacity of the installation as in the case of the famous 800 MW casus, as mentioned in the current version of the Act. Already working prosumers will be able to join the new system, but they will not be obliged to do so, acting within the framework of the so-called. a small three-pack, but also a subsequent change of decision will be possible in their case. The absence of a limit of 800 MW combined with guaranteed tariffs will make the prosumer fully able to decide when to install the installation, which is important e.g. at the construction of the house. Prosumer will be able to plan an investment in RES without pursuing with others for market share limited by a limit of 800 MW. Municipalities owned by many facilities will be able to plan investments in RES in the long term rather than on an action basis. Today it can already be said openly that tariffs were guaranteed a stage in the fight for energy distributed in Poland. Although adopted in February 2015, the previous PO-PSL team did nothing to ensure that the Act entered into force. It was enough to issue a few implementing regulations, but one month after the President signed the bill, the Ministry surprised the market with the necessary amendment, and then reminded itself that this chapter on auctions and prosumers should be sent for notification. Ladies and gentlemen, some of the questions have intertwined, so in some cases, if you don't mind, I'll try not to repeat myself. A number of questions concerned the micro-installation and state support, which Mrs Lenartowicz from the Platform started with the question of why support is for 10 years rather than 15, as is the case with other RES sources. Therefore, there will be no situation in which someone will be in legal uncertainty as regards the length of time involved, while the State will not be put in a situation in which, for example, the State will not be placed. The natural development of technology will lead us to maintain the same period for which the installation is financed in the current law each time. I would like to make it clear to you here that auctions are, in fact, subsidies for investment costs, and only payments are made on a cyclical basis, allowing entrepreneurs to become profitable. He must actually raise funds for investment from capital markets, from own resources, maybe from credit. Therefore, writing a specific date makes it easier to discuss with the sector that finances it. In the case of prosumer it is slightly different. We are dealing with the following situation here. We have indicated that prosumer activity is not a business activity. Therefore, we need to put the thesis of countries that say that this is not worthwhile in a specific context. Here we are not talking about cost-effectiveness in an economic sense, we are talking about the interest of the consumer, the natural person, possibly any organisation to make this kind of expenditure. And of course, at this point, if this isn't an economic activity, it's a comparison on a principle: I would normally buy energy for an amount of x, and I wouldn't have to buy it because I had some expense, some expense, some cost. And now you can actually try to compare it. People, individual people bear expenses e.g. for cars, they bear these expenses, using loans, for example. Cyclically they sell - so here we do not introduce anything new - and of course in some way they evaluate their expenditures in terms of accepted values. We have assumed that, since prosumer activity is not a business activity, it is not a profit-oriented activity, we cannot consider it in the same categories as business activities. Nothing prevents a natural person, institution or company, any legal person from starting an economic activity to any extent, including micro-installations. Moreover, they are even provided for in the RES Act, and in the amendment this is underlined, the possibility of simplified clearing - precisely so as not to carry out complex settlements here. We simply continue to see solutions to these problems under the Radio and Television Act. That's the first basic thing. The second basic thing, which also remains unchanged in relation to the current state, is the preservation of the current organisational formula. We cannot agree with the view that the fact that the media are currently operating in the form of single-member companies of the Treasury is a burden for them. We do not see the need to change this legal and organisational form. The Act contains a sufficiently strong blockade for possible problems related to the operation of the company, because these are special companies - they can neither fall, says the article. Article 26 (2) (b) 2 draft law, nor do they have to generate profit. So it's just the form of company that offers opportunities, for example, in terms of financing, there are also some other solutions that we think are not the worst, but are not classic companies that, for comparison, fabricate shoes or shoelaces. So we don't change that. We also do not change the clear links between public media and the National Radio and Television Council. We believe that it is important to leave the current way in which the authorities of these public media are appointed. It's not like that - I've said it before - that the contest is some kind of pathology. It's not like the contest is worse than the appointment, and it's not quite clear with which criteria. So we leave a way of appointing these authorities, a way of recommending candidates to supervisory boards, i.e. by academic authorities, statutory bodies of universities, collegial bodies, because it seems to us that it is not from the world of politics, but from the environment of public media that these supervisory boards should be constructed so that they can also hold competitions for board members. So that doesn't change. The legal and organisational formula itself remains unchanged, the role of the national council remains unchanged, the way public media authorities are elected remains unchanged. During the hearing, we talked about this very, very much. I ignore the fact that it turns out that this designed system will probably not be able to enter into force on 1 January. At the moment, television with its branches has probably 440 million zlotys. So this is a fundamental change in these proportions. Such a recording of this method of funding means that, with growing wealth, prosperity, GDP growth and expenditure, public media are, of course, also gaining. If public media are so important, they can be financed as well. Well, the government doesn't give anything, and in fact, Parliament can only pick up something, but there's certainly nothing that can take away from anyone, because that money is just... [[Are you sure? Can't the bill change?]] Parliament and the governments shall comply with the budget law. It is adopted by Parliament without changing this indicator. On the other hand, this money goes to the Public Media Fund, which is a parallel institution - I see colleagues from the cultural committee, so they will know what I'm talking about - the State Institute of Film Art. There is, of course, a board of directors in which there are persons appointed by the public authority, the Minister of Finance, the Treasury and culture, or otherwise it would not be possible to manage this money would face difficulties in implementing European directives. This is the board itself, and there is a twelve-man council which is a social body. This advice works in sessions, it is not a permanent job, and it examines specific requests for allocation of these funds. The Council consists of representatives of creative, civic, i.e. non-governmental, local and local organisations. The distribution of this money or the allocation of this money, I would say, is absolutely the work of the social body, of course, with great responsibility, with reports that the Council gives to Parliament and to the national council. This report is part of the annual report of the National Council. It is a transparent system, transparent, everything is done again at the open courthouse. We know when the session is, when the assignment is, what were the conclusions, what applications and how it was decided. The Public Media Fund is a new body, I admit, but it seems to me that such a body, which here greatly socializes this process of managing public media, because, however, whoever gives money, certainly has some significant influence on how the institution that he gives money works. As these public funds have already increased significantly, we also propose some exemptions from the advertising activities of the media. It made us a little nervous. So this kind of channel. I don't think they should be created for making money, so that these channels are off. We also propose in one of the nationwide programs in two time intervals, i.e. in one time band 10 and in the second time band 16 and 22, after 3 hours of advertising silence. We've heard from a lot of TV viewers that we're tired of this advertising avalanche. Regardless of who it was and where it came from, from everyone, so to speak, television managers and programme builders, we hear complaints that valuable positions are in the middle of the night, because they do not bring advertising influence, and they race with these commercial stations. So if there's anything he's not gonna make, it's either in the middle of the night or it's a pale dawn, or you don't even know when. Also in prime time, so that there are no more complaints that we have to, that we can't. It's a time when there's no advertising by law, and then there can be very valuable programs. These are the main assumptions of this project. I'm not saying he's revolutionary, because we didn't mean revolution either.
