ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-19-sejm-19-2.u493	Mr. Marshal! Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! There are different public media funding systems in Europe, depending on the solutions adopted by individual countries. According to the European Union of shippers covering the countries of the European Union and the European Economic Area and selected non-EU countries, the most popular forms are the universal fee, i.e. the subscription fee that operates in 22 European countries, as well as the funding from the state budget in force in 15 countries. The subscription, which is a kind of universal fee effectively enforced and intended for public media tasks, seems to be the most mature way of financing public broadcasters. It can be said in simplification that it applies, with a few exceptions, especially in Western European countries with stable democracy such as England, France, Germany, Scandinavia, as well as the southern neighbours of Poland, the Czech Republic and Slovakia, which have recently decided to finance it - through a universal audiovisual fee or subscription fee. The advantages of such a fee are predictability, independence from governments and the creation of a form of link between media and their recipients. The form of funding based on the state budget is mainly present in the east of the continent. It is used by the Baltic States, Belarus, Ukraine, but also by Belgium, the Netherlands and Spain. It is a domain of countries with a young democracy or a small population. This form of public media power supply does not seem to ensure their stable functioning. The example of Spain, where public media are currently financed mainly by the state budget, shows that any crisis in the country's economy also has a negative impact on the financial situation of public media. Greece is an extreme example here, where the dramatic economic situation and the search for savings resulted in the liquidation of public media in 2013. True, they were created again under the influence of protests, but in a very limited form and still facing financial difficulties. [[Mr Iwona Śledzińska-Katarasińska: And now?]] This also points to the second weakness of budget funding, which is the high vulnerability of the media to the influence of the executive authority, in addition to instability. Projected funding of public service broadcasters equivalent to 0,3% in the proposed amendment State budget expenditure, Article. 32e paragraph. 1, which, according to the authors presented in the explanatory memorandum, would amount to just over a billion zlotys in 2016, will not result in... I remember what your messenger said in her speech that there might be more... [[Mr Iwona Śledzińska-Katarasińska: Yes, there is more.]] ...but on this example, which is in the justification, I will resist. This level of funding will not result in the de-commercialisation of the Polish Television programme, and will not become a necessary stimulus for all public media, because in recent years the National Council has passed on to the public media a little lower, 750 million PLN - although in 2016 it will be much less, it is another alarm bell, because the forecast says about 661 million PLN - and this 750 million PLN met the minimum needs of public radio, but not public television. In the document of the National Radio and Television Council of Poland, the new model of the functioning of public media in Poland indicated that after summing up the demand for television, Polish Radio and regional radio stations, taking into account the optimal way of carrying out statutory tasks of public broadcasters on the basis of the current legal state, this sum should ultimately amount to PLN 2680 million. When assessing the needs of broadcasters, they took into account the need for a reduction of revenues from commercial activities, mainly advertising, to at least 1/3 of the current advertising time, as indicated by the National Council. Moreover, as mentioned in the explanatory memorandum of the proposed amendment, the proposed system for financing public service broadcasters from the State budget is different from the financing system in force at the time of Poland's accession to the European Union and will therefore most likely require notification to the European Commission. According to the National Council, a period of several months - the proposed law would enter into force on 1 January of the following year - is too short a time for negotiations with the European Commission. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 16a. The 8th proposed amendment of the law was introduced in the Polish Television by limiting advertising messages, with the exception of self-promotion on one of the national antennas, eliminating advertising - this is what the rapporteur said - during the three hours of issue in the band between hours. 10 to 16 and during three hours of emissions in the time band between hours. 16 a 22. According to the National Council, the introduction of advertising restrictions in connection with stable and adequate public media funding is a legitimate demand. The designed solution introducing advertising restrictions in the Polish Television on one of the national antennas at certain times of the day does not seem to be the best idea. In the above-mentioned document, this new model of functioning of public media in Poland, the National Council considered all concepts of gradually reducing advertising in Polish Television: in individual programs, in individual months of the year, days of the week or times of the day. On TVP1 and TVP2 most of the advertising revenue is generated at the time of the biggest audience, it is so-called. prime time. This phenomenon is different in specialized programmes. Most of the revenue on these antennas brings out spots outside the time of the highest ratings. The solution of introducing different rules for advertising at times of the year, days of the week or times of the day or for various programmes - universal, specialized programmes - would, however, require the application of very complicated rules for negotiating advertising contracts and accounting for advertising time in designed frameworks. As a result of the very factual analyses carried out, it turned out that the most rational would be simply a uniform reduction in all programmes at the same time. With today's 12 mins the advertising time would be reduced in the first year to 8 min in the hour, in the second to 4 min in order to ultimately reach the level of up to 2.5 min, approx. 2,5 min in the third year, while maintaining the ban on interrupting the broadcasts by advertising of course. In the case of Polish Radio, advertising revenues no longer cover 20% of operating costs and are mostly generated in the main programs: Program 1 and Program 3. The total abandonment of advertising in Programmes 2 and 4 will be without significant impact on the commercial revenues of the sender. With regard to the One and the Three, the same solution is proposed as in the case of the Polish Television. The draft amendment to the law in Parliament proposes new regulations relating to the appointment of members of the Management Board and supervisory boards in a contest procedure, the detailed rules of procedure of which would be laid down by the national council by means of a regulation. These are changes that consistently improve the model functioning in the years 2010-2015. However, there is no way to supplement the composition of the company's bodies in the event of the creation of vacancies, e.g. as a result of the resignation from office. The National Council has formulated such proposals following the experience gained from two competitions with academics on the basis of the amendments introduced to the Radio and Television Act in 2010. The obligation to conduct a full competition procedure whenever the composition of the Supervisory Board needs to be completed may lead to temporary impediment to the functioning of the Board, since according to the wording of the draft law the Council must act in full composition. Following the 2011 competition, the National Council called for the possibility for members of the National Council to apply from among those who had previously received a recommendation from higher education institutions to be able to submit candidates during their term of office. In the section on public media tasks, in particular Article. 1, for the implementation of the public service mission, the project is largely limited to the current Radio and Television Act. Projectors have decided to remove from the current Article. Article 21 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 A law which identifies public service obligations, such elements as those which affect the performance of specific tasks affecting the richness and diversity of content and consequently the value and quality of the programmes, e.g. taking into account the needs of national and ethnic minorities and communities using regional language, tackling various types of pathologies, shaping health-promoting attitudes, promoting sport, taking action for education, in particular media, etc. Designed art. Article 21 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 contains the definition of a public service mission, but it is still general in nature and can be interpreted differently by both broadcasters and the assessment bodies. In the case of a fairly general definition in the draft public media tasks it appears necessary to clarify the public service mission by introducing a requirement to conclude with broadcasters so-called. duty cards, i.e. a kind of contract in force during the term of office of the Management Board. What is important and what I would like to stress is that the European Commission is currently assessing the statutory provisions proposed by Poland in this area and it seems that at the level of working talks this kind of agreement has actually been reached. He, of course, still needs confirmation by the new government and representatives of the new institutions, in particular the UOKiK, which conducts these negotiations. The Charter would set out the minimum shares of programmes performing missionary tasks in the field of information, publicity, culture, education and sport, including the level of new programming content, and the participation of the Prime Minister. The Charter would be bound by annual financial and programme plans, including investment and technological plans submitted to the National Council for evaluation and approval by resolution. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 3 of the Act amending the designer provides for the expiry of the existing Act of 21 April 2005 on subscription fees. According to Art. 9 Act on subscription fees The National Council of Radio and Television in exceptional situations, where specific social or random considerations so warrant, may cancel or depreciate arrears in payment of subscription fees in instalments. An entity required to pay subscription fees shall submit an application for redemption or distribution into instalments to the National Radio and Television Council. It appears that it is not appropriate that subscribers are deprived of the right to hear by the authority of the request for redemption or the distribution of payments into instalments where the creditor has established the existence of a arrears or the transfer of an enforcement title by way of enforcement proceedings. In addition, a number of cases relating to the redemption or distribution of claims on instalments have been challenged by subscribers to administrative courts, which also requires the courts to hear and perhaps, if so decided by the court, also to review by the administrative authority. The point is to review all these provisions concerning the so-called. pending cases relating to the redemption and payment of subscription fees into instalments so that they are completed in an appropriate manner by an administrative decision. Finally, I would like to highlight the main features of the parliamentary project in the assessment of the National Council for Radiophony and Television. The main advantage of this project is drawing from good and bad, but our, Polish, 25-year experience and achievements of the media. It seems that this project is in line with this principle, unlike previous projects that reject it, it seems, practically all the work to date, without examining the experiences of good and bad. Apart from the details of the solutions, the National Council welcomes in the project the trend of socialising media authorities with unchanged selection procedure, emancipation of public television branches, which will allow for further strengthening of regional media in line with the tradition of self-government in Poland. The weakness of the project is the budgetary method of financing the media. While research shows that it is gaining the greatest approval of Poles, the experiences of other countries say that in order for the media to play their social role well, they should be financed by citizens. To do this, however, one condition must be met: the media programme must be endorsed by society, its various environments and groups, it must be combined and not divided. This seems very difficult to achieve today. We proceed with the examination of item 20 of the agenda: Report of the Board of Administration and Home Affairs on the adoption of the Senate on the Act amending the Act on the granting of protection to foreigners in the territory of the Republic of Poland (prints Nos 511 and 519). I ask Mr Sylvester Tulayev to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-19-sejm-19-2.u531	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have a great honour and duty to present a report by the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs on the adoption of the Senate on the Act amending the Act on the granting of protection to foreigners in the territory of the Republic of Poland. Let us remind you very briefly that the law extends from 7 to 45 days the deadline to provide information about possible threats to the defence or security of the state or the protection of security and public order caused by possible entry and stay of a displaced or relocated foreigner in Poland. In particularly justified cases, that period may be extended by a further 14 days. The Act provides that resettlement or relocation will not be possible if the Head of the Office for Foreigners receives at least one information confirming the possibility of a threat to the defence or security of the State or the protection of security and public order. The Act also waives the obligation of the current presumption that failure to provide information within a period of time means that there is no such threat. The Senate examined the law at its meeting on 12 May 2016 and adopted a single amendment to it, which brings it into order, complements and harmonises the rules. Article 4 is replaced by the following: 86f paragraph. 4 Act on the granting of protection to foreigners in the territory of the Republic of Poland in the form of a bill under consideration provides that where the entry of a foreigner in the territory of the Republic of Poland and his stay in that territory may constitute a threat to the defence or security of the State or the protection of security and public order, the foreigner shall not be resettled. At the same time, this provision omits an equivalent decision in case of relocation, since it merely states that the Head of the Office for Foreigners informs the competent authority that the foreigner has not been qualified for relocation. In the Senate's view, it should be appropriate to correct the provision so that the lack of relocation, as in the case of resettlement, is directly due to the provisions of the Act. The Senate also considered that the wording of the Article was inadequate. 86f paragraph. 4, according to which the Head of the Office for Foreigners informs that the authority of the State from which the relocation is to take place is not eligible for relocation. If the foreigner is not qualified for relocation, the country from which the relocation was to take place may be notified, rather than the country from which the relocation is to take place. The Board of Administration and Home Affairs, after considering the Senate resolution at its meeting on 18 May 2016, recommends the adoption of the Senate amendment. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The second of these amendments is a purely technical amendment which indicates, where the procedure has actually been interrupted or a person has no right to enter the country, that in fact the reference must refer to the country from which the relocation was to take place and not to the country to which it is to take place, because the procedure is already closed there. For this reason, Kukiz15 will, of course, vote against the rejection of the Senate's amendments, i.e. in favour of their adoption. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-19-sejm-19-2.u538	Thank you very much. Mr Mirosław Suchoń, Modern.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-19-sejm-19-2.u539	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Poselski Club on the resolution of the Senate on the Act amending the Act on granting protection to foreigners on the territory of the Republic of Poland included in print No 511, the report of the committee is in print No 519. In the beginning, I would like to stress that the work on this bill was carried out in an atmosphere of full understanding for the essence of the change. I believe that in the case of laws affecting the level of security and the implementation of Poland's obligations to European partners, it is necessary to proceed in such a way that these are substantive and not side issues, as an area of discussion. That is exactly what happened, and I wanted to thank all the members of the committee for that. The amendment itself involves extending the deadline for the Head of the Office for Foreigners to consult the safety and public order authorities listed in the Act in relation to persons notified for relocation to Poland. This is the result of changes made by the Council of the European Union in such a way that the time for relocation is extended from one month to two months. As a result of the adoption of the amendment, the current 7-day time limit for consultation by the Head of the Office for Foreigners will be extended to 45 days, and in exceptional cases this consultation may last up to 59 days. However, the provisions introduced by the amending act will not apply to foreigners who have not completed 13. Year of age - this is the result of the European Union's rules. The law also introduces the principle that even one negative position of the authority to which the head of the Office for Foreigners asked for a foreigner's check will prejudice that the person will not be accepted by the Republic of Poland as part of the relocation. The Act adopted by the High House on 13 April 2016 was passed to the Senate and the Senate tabled one amendment. The amendment tabled by the Senate is orderly, complementary and uniform. In particular, it concerns the provision of the Article. 86f paragraph. 4 of the Act and corrects this provision so that the possibility of relocation, as it is in the case of resettlement, results from expressis verbis from the provisions of the Act. In addition, the Senate also considered the wording adopted by the Sejm to be inadequate in Article. 86f paragraph. 4, according to which the Head of the Office for Foreigners informs that the authority of the country from which the relocation is to take place is not eligible for relocation. If the foreigner is not qualified for relocation, then it is possible to notify the country from which the relocation was to take place, and so proposed the Senate in the amendment. Given the nature of the amendments, the Modern Parliamentary Club obviously supports the amendment tabled by the Senate, i.e. it will vote against [[♪ The bell ♪]] rejecting this amendment, as suggested by the committee. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-19-sejm-19-2.u543	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report by the Health Committee on the Senate's resolution on the Law amending the Law on Public Blood Service and some other laws contained in print No. 512. Marshal of the Sejm according to Art. 54 (1) (a) 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm on 13 May 2016 referred the Senate's resolution on the above matter to the Committee on Health for consideration. The Health Committee, having considered this resolution at its meeting on 18 May 2016, after considering the amendments tabled by the Senate, proposes that the High Sejm adopt amendments from 1 to 14 and amendment 9 should be considered together with amendment 12. Part of the amendments is more specific, these are amendments 3, 4, 10 and 11. Amendment 1 deserves a distinction because it was recognised that the transfer of blood donor data to the Polish Red Cross in order to issue a badge by this organization should take place only with the consent of the donors themselves because not every donor is interested in receiving such a badge. Amendment 2 states that it is not justified to maintain a marking order for blood packaging preventing the identification of donor and recipient of blood in the event of autologous donation. Amendment 5 introduces an appropriate definition for the type of tests referred to in the provision. Amendment 6 changes have been made to indicate that the rule applies to blood donors. Amendment 7 had to remove the erroneous reference in Article. 1 in point 18 in point (a) d and f act due to the fact that the Military Blood and Blood Centre and the Blood and Blood Centre of the Ministry of the Interior and Administration do not distribute blood products, recombinant coagulation factors concentrates and desmopressin. Amendment 8 considers it necessary to set deadlines for reporting by public blood services units, which will make the obligation more real. Amendment 9 should be supplemented by the provision of the Article. 27 Law on the Public Blood Service to clarify the task of the public blood service organisation to carry out the supply of blood products, recombinant concentrates of coagulation agents and desmopressin. The consequence of this amendment is amendment 12 supplementing the provisions of the Pharmaceutical Law to indicate that distribution of blood products, recombinant coagulation agent concentrates and desmopressin may take place for the purpose of an individual order. At the same time, Amendment 12 complements the Law on the rules on the assessment of the premises, the scope of activity and the working time of the hospital pharmacy unit established in the public blood service organisation. It was also decided to change the deadlines by which data to the e-blood system will be transmitted to the full extent - as regards Amendments 13 and 14. Ladies and gentlemen! The Health Committee proposes that the proposed amendments be adopted. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-19-sejm-19-2.u544	Thank you, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear three minutes' statements on behalf of the clubs and the circle. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Krzysztof Ostrowski, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-19-sejm-19-2.u545	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, On 10 December 2015, the European Commission brought an action before the Court of Justice of the European Union because of Poland's failure to comply with EU directives on blood donation and blood treatment. The government has prepared a draft law which implements into the Polish legal system of the European Union directive on: firstly, standards of quality, safe collection, testing, storage and distribution of blood and its components; secondly, a system of ensuring the safety of blood and its components, namely full traceability of blood and its components, from the blood donor to the recipient and vice versa; thirdly, rules of import of blood and its components from outside the Member States of the European Union, which were previously not regulated in Polish legislation; fourthly, the creation of an electronic system of e-blood containing information on blood donors, adverse reactions and adverse events. This system will also be used for reporting. Member States are obliged, inter alia, to do so. to submit an annual report on blood management. The other proposed changes, most importantly, are the harmonisation of the data that should include the legitimacy of the Honorary Blood Donor. In addition, the principles of applying for the title and badges of the Honorable Blood Giver have been amended. The scope of subjects entitled to apply to the Minister of Health for the donation of an honorary donor of at least 20 litres of blood, the Honorary Blood Giver - Deserving for the Health of the Nation has also been changed. The authorised entities are the head of the organizational unit of the competent public blood service and bodies of nationwide honorary blood donors associations. I would like to say that the time to introduce a law is 3 years, 2017/12019. The funds will be obtained from the European Union and the cost of implementing the law is estimated at PLN 120 million. As my colleague said at the committee meeting, the Senate amendments were adopted unanimously. Thank you.

The purpose of this Act, as we have repeatedly mentioned during the discussion, also today, is to transpose into Polish legislation the requirements of four European directives, including a directive laying down standards for quality and safety of collection, testing, preparation, storage and distribution of human blood and blood components, directives on certain technical requirements for blood and blood components, directives on blood traceability requirements and notification of serious adverse reactions and events, and directives on Community standards and specifications relating to the quality system in force in blood establishments. Act passed by the High House on 13 April this year. has been transferred to the Senate, while the Senate has tabled 14 amendments. Part of the Senate amendments is more precise, editorial or corrective, amendments 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 10 and 11. We wanted to see such a very good change, namely Amendment 1, in which the Senate considered that the transfer of blood donors' data to the Polish Red Cross in order to issue a badge by this organization should take place only with the consent of the donors themselves because not every donor is interested in receiving such a badge. It is so obvious, I would say, of course, but only the Senate decided to regulate it in this way. In Amendment 2. The Senate has come to the conclusion that it is not justified to keep an order for blood packaging that prevents the identification of the donor and recipient of blood in the event of autologous donation. It's like another obvious amendment, it seems to have to be agreed with. In Amendment 8. The Senate considered it necessary to set deadlines for reporting, which is also a positive change. Amendment 9, according to the Senate, had to be supplemented by the Article. 27. to clarify the task of the public-service bodies to carry out supplies. The consequence of this amendment is Amendment 12 concerning the distribution of these products and aspects related to the operation of hospital pharmacy. Amendments 13 and 14. The Senate decided to change the time limits [[♪ The bell ♪]] data to the system will be transferred to the full extent. Given the nature of these amendments, the Modern Club obviously supports these amendments tabled by the Senate, i.e. it will vote against rejection in accordance with the recommendation of the committee. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-19-sejm-19-2.u567	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Ostrowiec Świętokrzyski, second in terms of area and population center of Świętokrzyskie voivodship, has been known as a steel town for many years. The tradition of the steel industry goes back to a year here. 1813, when the owner of the Ostrowiece estate, Jerzy Dobrzański, founded the first steel factory in the village called Kuźnia, which is now within the city limits. During these more than 200 years, the steel industry had different fates, and the existing plants had different names. In the second half of the 19th century, their dynamic development was connected with the formation of the Great Piece and Ostrowice Works, which grew into the second largest steelworks in the Kingdom of Poland at the turn of the 19th and 20th centuries. The existence of such a thriving steel plant was of great importance to Ostrowiec, resulted in an increase in the number of its inhabitants and in the formation of further companies. The development of the steelworks did not stop the World War I event, although the destruction made by the Russian, Austrian and German troops was enormous. The rapid reconstruction of the plant led him back to the top among the companies of the Staropol Industrial District, transforming himself into a public limited company. The period of World War II brought further damage due to the methodical looting of steel machines and equipment. Nearly 3,000 were transported to Germany. wagons loaded with machinery and raw materials, and losses reached 80% The property of the steelworks. What the Germans failed to transport was blown up. The losses would have been even greater if it hadn't been for the attitude of the Ostrowiec steel workers who had hidden some of the machines and tools at risk of their lives. As a result, production was partially started as early as 1945. The period after World War II is the time of the development of the steelworks named Marcel Nowotka. This development was symbolized by the construction of a new metallurgical plant, which started operating in 1972. The size of the steelworks during this period is evidenced by the fact that in 1981 it employed more than 17.5 thousand. employees. In the 1980s, the situation of the plant was difficult, and this was influenced, among others. shortages of raw materials and materials. It deteriorated further after 1989 when it turned out that Polish steel industry, technologically backward, was unable to compete on the free market. The 1990s were therefore a time of struggle for survival, caused by the extremely difficult financial situation of the steelworks. It led to its bankruptcy in July 2002, but the bankruptcy fortunately did not mean liquidation. Production continued and in 2003 the steelworks were purchased by the Spanish Celsa group and until today functioned as Celsa Huta Ostrowiec SA. The emergence of a new investor, a new owner meant big investments and made the plant a modern company producing, among others. Rolled products, monolithic crankshafts for diesel engines and folding shafts for marine engines. At this point it is worth asking yourself: Does this mean that the company's situation is perfect? No, I'm afraid not. Unfair competition from producers from Ukraine, Belarus and China in particular and the very high costs of electricity used for steel production, much higher than in many European countries, make the situation of Polish steelworks not one of the best. The prospect of implementing EU climate policy directives also raises concern. Their implementation is threatened by the fact that after 2020 the mills can be closed. High Seym, May is the time when Polish steelmen celebrate their holiday. Once the days of the steelworks in Ostrowiec were celebrated throughout the city. Today they have a much more modest character. On the occasion of this holiday, I would like to make my best wishes, to wish them well and to wish them well. I wish their hard and responsible work would always be appreciated. I wish the Celsa Directorate-General to develop and overcome existing difficulties. I support this action and will continue to support it not only as a sharpshooter. [[♪ The bell ♪]] A parliamentarian, but also as a grandson and son of a steel worker. God bless the steel work! Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

It is very simple: real integration. A disabled person in urban space can function equally with a full-time person, including a bus, a shop, on the street. As most often appear in the space of the city, get used to differentness, disenchant your image, overcome indifference or fear. The aim is to make the good part of society aware that it is important not to be afraid of the disabled. On the contrary, it is time and time to get to know him a little better. The game is supposed to break patterns, stereotypes, teach mutual tolerance. The project is based on close cooperation between schools and institutions and local companies. By using public transport properly, he is to promote proper behaviour on buses and to teach mutual kindness. Through the historical thread he is to arouse love for a small homeland, and the motives of multiculturalism in our territories are to deepen respect for another man. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-19-sejm-19-2.u577	Thank you kindly. Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Ladies and gentlemen! Tonight, for this brief moment, I will return to history. After all, history is not only a past time, it is realized in the lives of nations, and under no circumstances can it be separated from the present. It is a kind of value of history carried over generations to the next. Today's very sad story is an image of the fragility of human existence, in this case depending on the invader, which were German and Soviet invaders during World War II. The tragedy of these events took place on 28 February, 10 May and 20 May 1942, and the burden of my family's experiences on my land was permanent in the memory and conscience of people, bearing the experience and picture of the great harm done in our nation. This year there is another 74th anniversary of the pacification of the village in the Dwarf and Zamość district. This pacification was an organized armor campaign against the civilian population, which aimed to intimidate and discourage the conspiracy struggle in the resistance. Head of the Gestapo in this area Kurt Engels and his Deputy Ludwig Klemm It was in February and May 1942 that they decided to deal with the population living in the villages of Orłów Drewniany, Kryniczki, Wiszenki, Anielpol, involving significant forces of German police and Ukrainian troops in German service, so-called. The navy police. The action began with arson of the village, not allowing anything to be saved. So all his possessions were lost in the fire. Those who moved by flames or by burning buildings were fired from precincts. In the order of death, innocent civilians were selected and executed without sacrificing the elderly and children. 9-year-old Mary Hawrylak of Wiszenek died with a toy in her hand. Young boys were ordered to dig a hole into a common grave, and they were treated with bullets. The inhabitants of our villages were also killed by entire families on their farms. That's how Anastasia Water and her two sons Janek and Tadzio were killed. Other young boys were strapped to their hands and chased to a waiting transport to take them to the camp in Majdanek. Many of them never came back. This crime was undoubtedly a punishment for cooperating with a partisan from nearby forests. Kurt Engels and Ludwig Klemm in their cruelty were so perverse that they arranged for mass executions of so many human beings, as they themselves described, to have a good time. At that time, in its dispatch to London on June 3, 1942, a government delegate to Cyril Rataiski informed about mass executions of civilians in these districts, listing three villages: Eagles, Krynicki and Wiszenki. In addition to pacification, Zamość is also a mass displacement. The first attempts were already made on 6 November 1941. On that day Poles were suddenly expelled from the village of Huszczka Mała and Huszczka Wielka. The residents were given 20 minutes to pack their luggage. The main wave of displacement began on 28 February 1942, and lasted until March 1943. Between 3 and 8 December 1942, the village of Wirkowice in my municipality of Izbica was displaced. During this period 117 villages were displaced. In total, more than 40,000 were displaced as part of the operation. persons. All resettlement activities were extremely brutal and were a politically intended movement for the settlement of their own. Those who were strong and healthy were then sent to the Reich, weak to the camp in Oświęcim. The tragic fate did not miss the youngest - the children of Zamość. Children were forced away from their families and placed in barracks with barbed wire. There was no talking about seeing your parents. Many times the desire to see children has been paid for by death. Over 30,000 were transported during the occupation. Children to the Reich. The tragic picture of such great suffering presented in these few sentences should be remembered forever. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-19-sejm-19-2.u597	A statement by the Members on the 114th anniversary of Jan Kiepura's birth. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! May 16th this year We celebrated the 114th anniversary of the birth of Jan Kiepura, a Polish singer and actor, enjoying great international popularity and fame, with numerous successes on the boards of world theatres and cinema screens. Jan Viktor Kiepura was born in Sosnowiec. He was the son of Francis, the baker, and musically gifted Maria from Neuman's house. Between 1916 and 1920 he attended the State Secondary School. Stanisław Staszic in Sosnowiec, formerly known as the Men's School of Commerce, where he graduated. As a student, he presented his vocal talent by singing opera arias on the dorms. During World War I, Kiepura was a member of the Polish Military Organization. In 1919, as a volunteer, he joined 1. The Bytom Shooting Regiment where he became an intelligence officer. A year later he and his father and brother took part in the First Silesian Uprising. Shortly after his graduation, after his father's command, Kiepura signed up for the Faculty of Law at the University of Warsaw. In secret, however, he learned solo singing with Professor Wacław Brzeziński. He also took lessons from the famous tenor of Milan's La Scala - Tadeusz Kopystyński. After 3 years of singing, Professor Brzeziński recommended Kiepura to the Great Theatre in Warsaw. After interrogation at the opera, he became his adept, neglecting his law studies, and finally giving them up altogether. For his insubordination and his voice pierced by the choir, he was also removed from the opera by the director. Emil Mlynarski. So he returned to Sosnowiec, where he met Professor Ignacy Warmuth. Thanks to this acquaintance he started singing in Lviv and then in Poznań Opera. He was a great success there, so he decided to return to Warsaw and undertake another attempt to work at the opera. Director Emil Młynarski accepted him again, this time as a soloist. Kiepura had many successes in Halce, Faust or Scary Court. In 1926. Kiepura went to Vienna, Paris and Milan. Four years later, his great film career began. His first movies were Neapol, singing the city and Song of the Night. On one of the film plans Kiepura met a singer and actress of Hungarian origin Marta Eggerth, with whom he married after 2 years of acquaintance. They went to New York together. They had two sons. The concerts of Kiepura were attended by representatives of consulates, embassies and diplomatic corps. For those who couldn't afford tickets, Kiepura sang from the balconies of the hotels in which he lived, or even from the rooftops of the cars. It quickly became a symbol of Polish music culture. Kiepura wanted to visit Poland very much, but he was not granted a visa to visit his father's funeral. Moreover, in the Polish press under communist censorship about Kiepura there were fatal reviews, and the newspapers tried to defame him. In June 1966, the Council adopted Regulation (EU) Kiepura signed a deal with the Metropolitan Opera in New York. At that time he began rehearsing for the opera "Carmen" Bizet. Unfortunately, the sudden death of an artist caused by a heart attack interrupted this undertaking. Kiepura was buried, as he wished, in the cemetery of Powęzkowski in the Alley of the Deserved. The funeral gathered about 200,000. persons. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-19-sejm-19-2.u598	Ladies and gentlemen! High Izbo! I'd like to dedicate my speech today to the amazons. Of course, not the famous Greek mythology, but the modern one. They can appreciate life as much as anyone can. They're grateful for every ray of sunshine, every breath of wind that we can't even see every day. We're going through whatever we have. What we should appreciate and what we should be grateful for. Amazons take care of what's important and don't waste time on unnecessary arguments or sorrows. Time will pass anyway, so it's worth making the most of it. We often forget that we have one life, there is no counter that sets the date for the end of our term in this world. Amazon-Dana Association in Radom on 29 April this year. They celebrated the 20th anniversary of their existence. 20 years of support, meetings, tears and laughter. No one can understand the woman who won the fight against cancer as well as the other woman who survived the same thing. Unfortunately, the number of women with cancer is not decreasing. To realize that this isn't the end, that it's the beginning of a struggle, after which life will go on. Radom's Amazon-Dana Association was founded on the initiative of Dr Danuta Krajewska, who is very committed to supporting the association. On the occasion, I would like to congratulate and thank the initiator, the aforementioned Dr Danuta Krajewska, and the founders and co-workers, ladies: Krystyna Klochowicz, Halina Nosel, Barbara Urbanska, Theodore Okoń, Teresa Wojciechowska. The association has implemented numerous educational and rehabilitation programs over the years. The institution is a rock for post-mastectomy women who have won the fight against cancer. The loss and scarring of women suffering from breast cancer will remind them of this drama for the rest of their lives. Therefore, at such moments it is good to know that they have together the Amazon-Dana association in Radom, where women are working ready to provide support and professional care.
